g_{k}^{i}
’s are independently drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
— independently of the randomness in the system, as a means to ensure the distributional characterization in Lemma
1
.
Towards bounding this quantity, recognizing that
\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{t-1}\}
forms an orthonormal basis, there exist a unit vector
\mu_{t}=[\mu_{t}^{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
where

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{k}\gamma_{t}^{i}q_{i}^{k}-\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\Big]\Big\|_{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\gamma_{t}^{i}q_{i}^{k}-\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\Big).

Therefore, this quantity can be handled via standard Gaussian concentration inequalities as detailed in
(
Li and Wei, 2022
, Lemma 3)
.
We now state the result directly without repeating its proof. With probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, it satisfies

\displaystyle\xi_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t}(\beta_{t})\right\rangle-\langle F_{t}^{\prime}(\beta_{t})\rangle\alpha_{t-1}^{k}\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\right).

(71)

By similar analysis, we are also ensured that with probability at least
1-O(p^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}b_{k}\left[\left\langle\phi_{k},G_{t}(s_{t})\right\rangle-\langle G_{t}^{\prime}(s_{t})\rangle\gamma_{t}^{k}\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\right)

(72)

holds true.

Establishing the expansions (
53
).

Let us start with the term
\xi_{t}
. For every
t\geq 0
, first recall that

\displaystyle\xi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})\right\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle\right]}_{=:\mathcal{R}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\right).

Intuitively, the magnitude of
\mathcal{R}_{1}
is determined by the difference between
\beta_{t+1}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
. If the expansion (
53b
) were true, the difference between
\beta_{t+1}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
arises from
\widehat{\zeta}_{t}
as well as the difference between
\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(\beta_{k})
versus
\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(v_{k})
.

Next, if we project the term
\mathcal{R}_{1}
to the direction that aligns with vector
G_{t}(s_{t})
and its orthogonal linear space, we end up with decomposition

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}\cdot G_{t}(s_{t})^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{1}\cdot G_{t}(s_{t})+\mathcal{P}_{G_{t}(s_{t})}^{\perp}\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=G_{t}(s_{t})\cdot\frac{1}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\left[\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle\right]+\mathcal{P}_{G_{t}(s_{t})}^{\perp}\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle\right],

where the first equality uses property
\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}
and the second equality follows from the expansion (
63
).
In addition, due to the property of the orthogonal projection, we also find

\displaystyle\left\langle G_{t}(s_{t}),\mathcal{P}_{G_{t}(s_{t})}^{\perp}\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}a_{k},\mathcal{P}_{G_{t}(s_{t})}^{\perp}\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle\right]\right\rangle=0.

(73)

Putting the pieces above together,
\xi_{t+1}
admits the following expression

\displaystyle\xi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=G_{t}(s_{t})\cdot\frac{1}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\Big\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{P}_{G_{t}(s_{t})}^{\perp}\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\right),

(74)

Contrasting what we have shown above with our target, it is sufficient to consider the second term above, which shall be done as follows.

In the following, we establish the claim by decomposing the second term into two parts, corresponding to the influence of
\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(\beta_{k})
’s and the randomness of Onsager term, respectively.

To simply the notation, let us define

\displaystyle A_{t}^{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}-\sum_{j=k+1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{j}\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right)\right\rangle\alpha_{j-1}^{k}.

In view of this piece of notation and for each
i\geq 1
,
\sum_{k=1}^{i}a_{k}\alpha_{i}^{k}=G_{i}(s_{i})
, a little algebra leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}+\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left[\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\alpha_{t}^{k}+\sum_{j=k+1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{j}\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right)\right\rangle\alpha_{j-1}^{k}+A_{t}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=G_{t}(s_{t})\cdot\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{k}G_{k}(s_{k})+\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}A_{t}^{k}.

(75)

Here, the last relation follows from

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{j}\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right)\right\rangle\alpha_{j-1}^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\sum_{j=k+1}^{t}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{j-1}\alpha_{j-1}^{k}a_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{t}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{j-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\alpha_{j-1}^{k}a_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{k}G_{k}(s_{k}),

where we remind the readers that
\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{k}
is defined as of expression (
–
‣
•
‣
3.1
).
Taking (
A.2
) collectively with (
A.2
) and recognizing the definition of
\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{t}
, we end up with

\displaystyle\xi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{t}G_{t}(s_{t})+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{k}G_{k}(s_{k})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}A_{t}^{k}+\mathcal{P}_{G_{t}(s_{t})}^{\perp}\sum_{k=1}^{t}a_{k}\left\langle\psi_{k},F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})-F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\rangle+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\right),

which validates the expansion (
53a
) for
t+1
. It is also worth noting that

\widehat{\xi}_{t+1}
is defined exactly as the sum of the last three terms above.

When it comes to the expansion (
53b
) at
t+1
, repeating a symmetric argument above leads to the required result. We omit its proof for brevity.

A.3
Proof of Theorem
5

In order to prove this result, let us first state a key auxiliary lemma.

Lemma 2
.

Under the decomposition (
22
) with (
53
) and Assumption
1
, the Claim
1
, stated below, holds for
t=1
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.
In addition, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, for every

\displaystyle 1<t\lesssim\frac{n}{\log^{4}n},

(76)

if the Claim
1
, Assumption
1
and
2
all hold true for
t
, then Claim
1
holds for
t+1
.

The proof of this result can be found in Section
D
.

Claim 1
.

There exists universal constant
0<c<1
, such that
the following set of inequalities hold true

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\xi}_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\widehat{\zeta}_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}},

(77a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{t-1}^{t-1}\lesssim\left(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{t}\lesssim\left(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},

(77b)

\displaystyle|\widehat{\alpha}_{t-1}^{k}|\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}(1-c)^{t-k-1}\Big|\widehat{\alpha}_{\frac{t+k-1}{2}}^{\frac{t+k-1}{2}}\Big|&\text{if}&t-1-k=2m,\\[11.38092pt]
(1-c)^{t-k-2}\rho_{F}^{2}\Big|\widehat{\gamma}_{\frac{t+k}{2}}^{\frac{t+k}{2}}\Big|&\text{if}&t-1-k=2m+1,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}(1-c)^{\frac{t-k}{2}}\Big|\widehat{\gamma}_{\frac{t+k}{2}}^{\frac{t+k}{2}}\Big|&\text{if}&t-k=2m,\\
(1-c)^{\frac{t-k}{2}}\rho_{G}^{2}\Big|\widehat{\alpha}_{\frac{t+k-1}{2}}^{\frac{t+k-1}{2}}\Big|&\text{if}&t-1-k=2m.\end{array}\right.

Based on this lemma, we first make the observation that if Claim
1
holds true at iteration
t
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}|\widehat{\alpha}_{t-1}^{k}|\|G_{k}(s_{k})\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\xi}_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}|\widehat{\alpha}_{t-1}^{k}|\cdot\|\alpha_{k}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}}\lesssim\left(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(78a)

Here the first line invokes the relation that
\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}
, and the second line uses the inductive assumption (
77a
) and the geometric decay of
\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{k}
in expression (
77
).
Similarly, we can deduce

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(78b)

Now if Assumptions
1
and
2
hold true over the execution of the AMP iterations,
Claim
1
is established by induction, since Lemma
2
validates both the initial condition and the inductive argument for Claim
1
.

Appendix B
Proof for sparse and robust regression

B.1
Proof of Theorem
2

The proof of this result is built upon Theorem
5
.
To show the residuals satisfy relation (
34
), it is sufficient to validate Assumptions
1
and
2
over the execution of the AMP iterations.
We leave the arguments about the state evolution to Section
B.1.3
.

B.1.1
Validating Assumption
1

First, we make the direct observations that
F_{t}
and
G_{t}
defined in (
26
) satisfy
\rho_{F_{t}},\rho_{G_{t}}=1
and
\rho_{1,F_{t}},\rho_{1,G_{t}}=0

and
\left\|F_{t}(0)\right\|_{2},\left\|G_{t}(0)\right\|_{2}\lesssim 1
given
\|{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2},\|\varepsilon\|_{2}\asymp 1.

Then it is sufficient to verify that with high probability,

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\asymp\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1.

(79)

Towards this goal, recalling the initial choice where

\displaystyle\beta_{1}=-{\theta}^{\star}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad s_{1}=Y-\varepsilon,

and the norm property (
23
), we find
\|\gamma_{1}\|_{2}=\|F_{1}(\beta_{1})\|_{2}=\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}
.
In addition, notice that if
\tau_{t}
is selected to be
\infty
, we observe

\displaystyle\|r_{t}\|_{2}=\|\varepsilon+X^{\top}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\lesssim 1.

As
\tau_{t}
is selected as the one that minimizes
\|r_{t}\|_{2}
, it implies that

\displaystyle\Big\|\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}+\xi_{t}\Big\|_{2}\lesssim 1.

(80)

Consequently, regarding
\alpha_{t}
, we bound

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\Big\|\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}+\xi_{t}\Big\|_{2}\lesssim 1.

(81)

To further control the right hand side above, it is helpful to notice that

\displaystyle\Big\|\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}+\xi_{t}\Big\|_{2}=\Big\|\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}\Big\|_{2}+O(\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}),

and with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\varepsilon^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{j}\phi_{j}

\displaystyle=\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\right)\left\|\gamma_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}\left\|\gamma_{t}\right\|_{2}\Big),

(82)

where the last inequality invokes the spectral property as in (
172a
).
Putting everything together, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2})+\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\right).

(83)

Together with the relation (
81
) and
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}\asymp 1
, the relation above
leads to

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1,\qquad\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1.

(84)

Finally, let us establish a proper lower bound for
\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}
.
Again, as a consequence of the norm relation (
23
) and the Lipschitz property of soft-thresholding function, we write

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}=\|F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}\Big(\theta^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}+\zeta_{t}\Big)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}\Big(\theta^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}\Big)\Big\|_{2}+O\Big(\|\zeta_{t}\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\big\|_{2}\mid\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\Big]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\|\zeta_{t}\|_{2}\right).

(85)

Here, we invoke standard concentration inequality for Lipschitz function of Gaussian random variables
(
Borell, 1975
)
.
To accommodate the randomness in
\alpha_{t}\in\real^{t}
, we take a union bound over a covering set of
\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
of accuracy
\frac{1}{n}
.
Putting these ideas together, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, it is ensured that

\displaystyle\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\theta^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{j}\psi_{j}\Big)\Big\|_{2}-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\Big\|_{2}\mid\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\Bigg]=O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big).

(86)

Hence, it suffices to bound the right hand side of (
85
) from below which shall be down as follows.
Towards this goal, for
\mu\asymp 1
, independent of
g
, if we define event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}:=\left\{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p})~|~\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\big\|_{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\big\|_{2}\mid\mu\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\right\},

(87)

as discussed above, the event
\mathcal{E}
happens with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.
In view of this set, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\big\|_{2}\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\|g\|_{2}^{2})\ind(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-O\left(n^{-10}\right)\right)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\big\|_{2}\right]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\left(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}})\log n+\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\|g\|_{2}^{2})\ind(\|g\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\log n)\right]\right),

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{ii})}}{{=}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\big\|_{2}\right]\right)^{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right),

(88)

Here (i) uses the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\mu g)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\theta}^{\star}\|^{2}+\|\theta^{\star}+\mu g\|^{2}\lesssim 1+\|g\|_{2}^{2},

by recognizing
\|{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}\asymp
and
\mu\asymp 1;

(ii) invokes the basic relation for Gaussian random variable where
\mathbb{E}[\|g\|_{2}^{2}\ind(\|g\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\log n)]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
for
g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p}).

Let us proceed to controlling the size of
\mathbb{E}[\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}]
which in turn,
provides the control of quantity
\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\big\|_{2}
.
We claim that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\gtrsim 1.

(89)

In the following, we prove the above claim by diving into two different cases and considering them separately.

•

First consider the case when
\tau_{t+1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\tau_{t+1}<\frac{\|\theta^{\star}\|_{1}}{4k}\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}\|{\theta}^{\star}\|_{2}}{k}\asymp\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},

(90)

for
\|{\theta}^{\star}\|_{1}
obeying (
33
) and
n>2k\log(p/k)
.
In this case, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\circ\ind(\theta^{\star}=0)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\||g|\ind(|g|\gtrsim 2\tau_{t+1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\gtrsim 1.

•

On the other hand, when the relation (
90
) is violated, we make the observation that

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\star}\|_{1}\leq 2\tau_{t+1}k+\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\sqrt{\|\ind(|\theta^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t+1})\|_{0}},

which, together with (
33
), gives

\displaystyle\|\ind(|\theta^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t+1})\|_{0}\gtrsim k.

Based on this property, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\tau_{t+1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\ind(|\theta^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t+1})\circ\ind(\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}|g|<\tau_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\gtrsim 1,

where in the last inequality, we make the observation that
\tau_{t+1}>\|{\theta}^{\star}\|_{1}/4k\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
and
\mathbb{P}(\ind(\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}|g_{i}|<\tau_{t+1}))=O(1).

Combining (
85
), (
88
) and (
89
) leads to

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\gtrsim 1,\qquad\text{for }t\lesssim\frac{n}{\log n}.

(91)

Taking this together with (
84
), we have completed the proof of (
79
)
and thus justified Assumption
1
.

B.1.2
Validating Assumption
2

It is easily seen that
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left\|G_{t}^{\prime}(u_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}=1
.
Therefore validating Assumption
2
is equivalent to validating

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left\|\ind\left(|\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|\geq\tau_{t+1}\right)\right\|_{0}<(1-2c)^{2},

(92)

for some constant
0<c<1/2.

To establish this result, we find it helpful to first make the observation that for some small constant
c^{\prime}>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\coloneqq\left\{\tau:-c^{\prime}\sqrt{k}<\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}<c^{\prime}\sqrt{k}\right\}\subset\mathcal{S}

(93)

with set

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\Big\{\tau:\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\ind\left(|(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)|\geq\tau\right)\right\|_{0}\Big]<(1-2c)^{2}n\Big\}.

Let us take the relation (
93
) as given for the moment, and come back to its proof at the end of this section.
Based on this result, we shall prove Claim (
92
) by showing that

\displaystyle\tau_{t+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}.

(94)

Proof of Claim (
94
).

We first prove that, if
\tau_{t+1}\notin\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
, it must satisfy

\displaystyle\inf_{\tau\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}}|\tau_{t+1}-\tau|>c^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},

(95)

for some constant
c^{\prime\prime}>0.

Here let us define an auxiliary set

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\left\{\tau:-\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}\sqrt{k}<\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}<\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}\sqrt{k}\right\}.

(96)

In order to see this, observe that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\langle\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g),\mathrm{sign}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\ind(|\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|>\tau)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\langle\tau-\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g\mathrm{sign}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g),\ind(|\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|>\tau)\right\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\theta_{i}^{\star}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\tau-\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right)\ind(|\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|>\tau)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad-(p-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|)\right].

(97)

The density function
|p_{\theta^{\star}_{i}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g_{i}}|\lesssim\sqrt{n}
for
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1
and
g_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
, and therefore the right hand is a
O(n)
-Lipschitz function of
\tau
. Consequently, for
\tau\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}\sqrt{k}\leq\Big|\nabla_{\tau_{t+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}-\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big|\lesssim n\inf_{\tau\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}}|\tau_{t+1}-\tau|,

which proves the claimed gap (
95
) between
\tau_{t+1}
to
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
.

Given the relation (
95
), for
\widehat{\tau}=\inf\{\tau\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime},\tau>\tau_{t+1}\}
, this further implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\inf_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\widehat{\tau}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{\gtrsim}}c^{\prime}\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\tau}-\tau_{t+1})\geq c^{\prime}\sqrt{n}\cdot\inf_{\tau\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}}|\tau_{t+1}-\tau|\gtrsim 1,

(98)

where (i) is a consequence of the mean value theorem.
Next we show that since
\tau_{t+1}
is selected to minimize
\|\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t+1}\gamma_{t+1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t}\|_{2}
, the above relation contradicts with the choice of
\tau_{t+1}
.
More specifically, as is shown in (
83
), (
85
) and (
88
), we can write

\displaystyle\Big\|\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t+1}\gamma_{t+1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\|\xi_{t+1}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\|\xi_{t+1}\|_{2}\right)}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\|\xi_{t+1}\|_{2}\right).

(99)

The threshold
\tau_{t+1}
therefore cannot satisfy (
B.1.2
), as otherwise it does not minimize
\|\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t+1}\gamma_{t+1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t}\|_{2}
, which in turn, validates the claimed relation (
92
).

Proof of Property (
93
).

It can be seen from numerical calculations (see Figure
1
and the discussions around inequality (
122
)) that for
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, if

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\tau)\Big]-(p/k-1)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]\in(-c,c).

(100)

we have

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1+(\frac{p}{k}-1)\mathbb{P}\left(|G|\geq\omega\right)}{2\log\frac{p}{k}}>0.

(101)

The above result tells us that, for all
\tau
satisfying

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\theta_{i}^{\star}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tau-\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right)\ind(|\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|>\tau)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-(p-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|)\right]\in(-c^{\prime}\sqrt{k},c^{\prime}\sqrt{k}),

(102)

which implies that for
\omega:=\frac{\sqrt{n}\tau}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}}
,

\displaystyle k\sup_{\theta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\ind(|\theta+G|>\tau)\right]-(p-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]\in(-c^{\prime}\sqrt{nk}/\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2},c^{\prime}\sqrt{nk}/\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}),

then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|\geq\tau\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(|G|\geq\omega\right)\leq\frac{2(1-4c)^{2}\log\frac{p}{k}-1}{\frac{p}{k}-1},

(103)

which establishes the property (
93
) immediately.
In order to see this, plugging in
n>2k\log\frac{p}{k}
, inequality (
103
) ensures

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|\geq\tau\right)<\frac{(1-4c)^{2}n-k}{p-k},

and hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\ind\left(|(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)|\geq\tau\right)\right\|_{0}\Big]\leq k+(p-k)\mathbb{P}\left(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|\geq\tau\right)<(1-4c)^{2}n.

Upper bound for
\tau_{t+1}
.

Finally, let us establish a property of
\tau_{t+1}
. Specifically, we shall prove that
\tau_{t+1}\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}
when
p/k\lesssim 1
.
Before proceeding, let us make the following two observations both of which result from direct Gaussian integral.
For
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, it satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{\omega}^{\infty}x\exp\Big(-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\Big),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\right)\circ\ind(\omega-|\theta_{i}|<G<\omega+|\theta_{i}|)\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\right)\circ\ind(G>\omega+|\theta_{i}|)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{|\theta_{i}|}x\exp\Big(-\frac{(x-\omega)^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}x\exp\Big(-\frac{(x+\omega)^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\Big(-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\Big)\bigg\{\int_{0}^{|\theta_{i}|}x\exp(\omega x)\exp\Big(-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x-2\int_{0}^{\infty}x\exp(-\omega x)\exp\Big(-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\Big(-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\Big)\bigg\{\int_{0}^{|\theta_{i}|}\frac{\omega^{2}x^{3}}{2}\exp\Big(-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x-2\int_{0}^{\infty}x\exp\Big(-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\Big(-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\Big)\Big[\omega^{2}\Big(1-\Big(\frac{\theta_{i}^{2}}{2}+1\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{\theta_{i}^{2}}{2}\Big)\Big)-2\Big].

Based on these two observations, for
\|\theta\|_{1}\gtrsim\sqrt{k}
and
\omega
large enough, it obeys

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\right]\gtrsim\omega^{2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\Big).

(104)

Now, considering the transformation
\omega=\sqrt{n}\tau/\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}
and
\theta_{i}=\theta_{i}^{\star}/\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\theta_{i}^{\star}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tau-\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right)\ind(|\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|>\tau)\right]-(p-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\right]-\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\frac{p-k}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right].

Taking the above together with (
104
), we have that given
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1

and
\tau>C/\sqrt{n}
for some constant
C
large enough,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}>c\sqrt{k},

for some constant
c>0
.
In view of the property (
94
), this property ensures that
\tau_{t+1}\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}
.
As a result, it also leads to

\displaystyle\omega\coloneqq\sqrt{n}\tau_{t+1}/\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]\gtrsim 1.

(105)

B.1.3
State evolution

Our final goal is to bound the difference between the non-asymptotic SE
(\alpha_{t},\gamma_{t+1})
to the deterministic SE defined in expression (
31
).
In the following, we shall use the induction method to achieve this goal.
In particular, it is easily validated that the set of relation (
35a
) holds true for
t=1
. Assuming that for some
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\big|\|\alpha_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}-\alpha^{\star 2}_{t-1}\big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{1/3}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big|\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\gamma^{\star 2}_{t}\big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{1/3},

(106)

it is thus sufficient to verify them for
t+1
.

Towards this end, let us first recall the expression (
83
) that

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right),

(107a)

where
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2},\|\varepsilon\|_{2}\asymp 1
.
In addition, combining expressions (
85
) and (
88
) yields

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\Big\|_{2}\mid\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\Bigg]+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\sqrt{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}}g)\Big\|_{2}\mid\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\Bigg]+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{ii})}}{{=}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}\big(\theta^{\star}+\sqrt{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}}g\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\mid\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\Big]\Big)^{1/2}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right).

(107b)

Here, for inequality
(\text{i})
, we have plugged in the relationship (
107a
) and invoked the Lipschitz property

\displaystyle\|{\theta}^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+(\omega+\Delta)g\|_{2}\leq\|{\theta}^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta^{\star}+\omega g)\|_{2}+\|\Delta g\|_{2}.

We remind the readers that vector
g\in\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p})
, and is independent with the
(\alpha_{t},\gamma_{t+1})
sequence.
For inequality
(\text{ii})
to hold, we recall the relation (
88
).
According to the optimality of
\tau_{t+1}
(in (
31
)), we also find

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})\|_{2}=\Big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(\theta_{t+1})\Big\|_{2}

(108)

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}^{\star}}\big(\theta_{t+1}\big)\big\|_{2}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}^{\star}}\big(\theta^{\star}+\sqrt{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}}g\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\mid\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\Big]\Big)^{1/2}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right),

(109)

where the last line is derived again by uniform concentration inequalities, similar to relation (
107
).

Armed with the recursive formula (
107
), controlling the difference between the non-asymptotic state evolution and its asymptotic analogue boils down to considering the growth of function

\displaystyle h_{\tau}(\mu):=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\sqrt{\mu}g)\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big],

(110)

for every value of
\tau>0
.
Direct computations yield

\displaystyle\big|h^{\prime}_{\tau}(\mu)\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau}(\theta^{\star}+\sqrt{\mu}g),-\ind(|\theta^{\star}+\sqrt{\mu}g|>\tau)\circ g\big\rangle\Big]\Big|.

(111)

Considering the new rescaling

\displaystyle\theta:=\sqrt{n}\theta^{\star}/\sqrt{\mu},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\omega:=\sqrt{n}\tau/\sqrt{\mu},

(112)

we can rewrite

\displaystyle h_{\tau}(\mu):=\frac{\mu}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\theta-\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta+G)\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big].

where
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
In terms of the new scaling, for
k
-sparse
{\theta}^{\star}
, some direct calculations lead to

\displaystyle\big|h^{\prime}_{\tau}(\mu)\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\theta-\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta+G),\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\circ G\big\rangle\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{k}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta+G)-\theta\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\big)G\Big]+\frac{p-k}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(G)G\big]\Big|.

(113)

We claim that there exists constant
c\in(0,1)
that only depends on the ratio
p/n
and
k/p
such that

\displaystyle\big|h^{\prime}_{\tau}(\mu)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta_{i}+G)-\theta\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\big)G\Big]+\frac{p-k}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(G)G\big]\Big|\leq 1-c,

(114)

for both
\tau=\tau_{t+1}
and
\tau=\tau_{t+1}^{\star}
.
Let us take this result as given for the moment and leave the proof of this claim to the end of this section.

Given this result, we can bound the difference

\displaystyle\big|\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\gamma_{t+1}^{\star 2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\tau=\tau_{t+1},\tau_{t+1}^{\star}}\big|h_{\tau}\Big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{\tau}\big(\gamma_{t}^{\star 2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\big)\Big|+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1-c)\big|\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big|+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right),

(115)

as well as

\displaystyle|\gamma_{t+1}^{\star 2}-\gamma_{t}^{\star 2}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\tau=\tau_{t}^{\star},\tau_{t+1}^{\star}}\Big|h_{\tau}\big(\gamma_{t}^{\star 2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{\tau}\big(\gamma_{t-1}^{\star 2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\big)\Big|\leq(1-c)\big|\gamma_{t}^{\star 2}-\gamma_{t-1}^{\star 2}\big|.

(116)

The last inequality ensures that sequence
\gamma_{t}^{\star}
converges to some fixed point
\gamma^{\star}
.
Recalling the initialization
\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{\star}_{1}=\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}
, invoking the relation (
B.1.3
) recursively leads to

\displaystyle|\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t+1}^{\star})^{2}|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Taking this together with (
107a
) ensures that

\displaystyle\big|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-(\alpha_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

In addition, the relation (
116
) ensures
\gamma_{t}^{\star}
converges to some
\gamma^{\star}
exponentially with
t.

Proof of Claim (
114
).

We start by proving the claim (
114
) for
\tau_{t+1}
. Let us recall that
\tau_{t+1}
satisfies inequalities (
94
). It thus yields

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i:\theta_{i}^{\star}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\tau_{t+1}-\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g)\right)\ind(|\theta_{i}^{\star}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|>\tau_{t+1})\Big]-(p-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}}(|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}g|)\right]\bigg|<c^{\prime}\sqrt{k},

for some small constant
c^{\prime}>0.

It is thus sufficient to consider
\tau_{t+1}
such that the above inequality holds true.
Letting
\omega:=\frac{\sqrt{n}\tau_{t+1}}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}}
, the above relation further leads to

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\Big]-(p/k-1)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]\bigg|<c^{\prime\prime},

(117)

for some small constant
c^{\prime\prime}>0
.
Here, we remind the readers that in Section
B.1.1
, we have shown
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1.

Recall that we assume
p>2.3k
and
n>2k\log\frac{p}{k}
.
To prove Claim (
114
), it is thus sufficient for us to show that for
e_{3},c_{3}
small enough,

\displaystyle 2.3<\frac{p}{k}=1+e_{3}+\frac{\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]},

(118)

it satisfies

\displaystyle\text{LHS}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{2\log\frac{p}{k}}\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta_{i}+G)-\theta\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\big)G\Big]+\frac{\frac{p}{k}-1}{2\log\frac{p}{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(G)G\big]\Big|<1-c_{3}.

(119)

It is easily seen that the above relation leads to the advertised bound (
114
) by recognizing
n>2k\log\frac{p}{k}
.

In order to prove the required inequality (
119
),
plugging the expression for
\frac{p}{k}
as in (
118
) and in view of the the concavity of
\log(\cdot)
,
we obtain

LHS

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\frac{\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta_{i}+G)-\theta\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\big)G+\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(G)G\big]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\right]\Big]}{2\sum_{i:\theta_{i}\neq 0}\log\Big(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta_{i}+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta_{i}+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\Big)}\Bigg|+e_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta}\Bigg|\frac{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta+G)-\theta\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\big)G+\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(G)G\big]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]\Big]}{2\log\Big(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\Big)}\Bigg|+e_{4}.

(120)

Let us define

\displaystyle H_{2}(\omega):=1-\sup_{\theta}\Bigg|\frac{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(\theta+G)-\theta\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\big)G+\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(G)G\big]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]\Big]}{2\log\Big(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\Big)}\Bigg|.

(121)

Figure
1
demonstrates that for some constant
c_{4}>0
,

\displaystyle H_{2}(\omega)>\min\{c_{4},~\frac{c_{4}}{\omega^{2}}\}.

Putting this together with the upper bound for
\omega
in (
105
) establishes (
119
), and thus the Claim (
114
) for
\tau_{t+1}
.

In addition, we make observations that
\tau_{t+1}^{\star}
satisfies

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\left\|\theta^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t+1}^{\star}}(\theta^{\star}+\alpha_{t}^{\star}g)\right\|_{2}^{2}=0,

and
\alpha_{t}^{\star}=\|\alpha_{t}^{\star}\|_{2}+o(1)
, which follows from (
107a
) and the induction condition.
As a result,
\tau_{t+1}^{\star}
also satisfies the relation (
94
), which by similar argument as above validates the Claim (
114
) for
\tau_{t+1}^{\star}
.

Figure 1:

Numerical calculations for
H_{1}(\omega)
and
H_{2}(\omega)
of (
123
) and (
121
) such that
p/k\geq 2.3
.

Proof of inequality (
101
).

Similarly, we make the observation that

\displaystyle\frac{1+\big(\frac{p}{k}-1\big)\mathbb{P}\left(|G|\geq\omega\right)}{2\log\frac{p}{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta}\frac{1+\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|G|\geq\omega\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}}{2\log\Big(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\Big)}+c_{4},

(122)

and define

\displaystyle H_{1}(\omega):=1-\sup_{\theta}\frac{1+\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|G|\geq\omega\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}}{2\log\Big(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\omega-G\mathrm{sign}(\theta+G)\right)\circ\ind(|\theta+G|>\omega)\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{ST}_{\omega}(|G|)\right]}\Big)}.

(123)

As a consequence, Figure
1
demonstrates the relation (
101
) through a similar argument as above.

B.2
Proof of Theorem
3

This result is again a consequence of Theorem
5
.
To establish the relation (
48
), we proceed by validating Assumptions
1
and
2
over the execution of the AMP iterations.
We derive the state evolution results in Section
B.2.3
.

B.2.1
Validating Assumption
1

Before proceeding, we make a remark that
b_{t}
is chosen according to (
44
) such that

\displaystyle p(1+1/b_{t})=\big\||r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\big\|_{0}=:k_{t}.

(124)

Such
b_{t}
exists since since when
b_{t}\to\infty
,
k_{t}=n>p=\lim_{b_{t}\to\infty}p(1+1/b_{t})
, and when
b_{t}\to 0
,
k_{t}\leq n<\infty=\lim_{b_{t}\to 0}p(1+1/b_{t})
.

Now we proceed to validate Assumption
1
in this case.
For denoising functions defined in (
45
), requirement (
57a
) satisfies straightforwardly.
In addition, we note that
\|F_{t}(0)\|_{2}=0
and

\displaystyle\|G_{t}(0)\|_{2}=\|g_{t}(\varepsilon)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\left\|\psi\left(\varepsilon;\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\|\varepsilon\|_{2}\lesssim 1,

Therefore, we only need to verify that
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\asymp\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1
.
We claim that this indeed the case.

Proof of
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2},\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
.

Specifically, if
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2},\|\zeta_{t-1}\|_{2}=o(1)
, we shall prove that there exists some large enough constant
\overline{\gamma}
with
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}/\overline{\gamma}<c
for some constant
c>0
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}<\overline{\gamma},\qquad\text{for every }t\geq 1.

(125)

It therefore implies
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
.
We begin by noticing that
\|\gamma_{1}\|_{2}=\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\lesssim\overline{\gamma}
.
In addition, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, one has

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\left\|\psi\left(s_{t}+\varepsilon;\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{p}\|s_{t}+\varepsilon\|_{2}=\frac{n}{p}\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t}+\varepsilon\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{p}\sqrt{\|\gamma_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2})+\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\Big),

(126)

where the last line follows from similar argument to (
83
).
In addition, invoking the spectral properties as in (
172
) again gives

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|F_{t+1}(\beta_{t+1})\|_{2}=\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}+\zeta_{t}\right\|_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{p}}\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}+\|\zeta_{t}\|_{2}\right).

(127)

If relation (
125
) does not hold, then there exists some
t
such that

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\geq\overline{\gamma}>\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}.

then as a consequence of (
B.2.1
) and (
127
), one has
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim\overline{\gamma}
.
By definition of
\overline{\gamma}
, this means
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}=o(1)
.
In the following, we show this is impossible.

In order to see this, let us first consider quantity
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}
.
Recognizing the Lipschitz property of the function
\|\psi(\cdot\mid\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{2}
, we write

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\left\|\psi\left(s_{t}+\varepsilon;\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big\|\psi\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{2}+O(\|\varepsilon+\xi_{t}\|_{2}).

(128)

Conditional on
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}
, we invoke the standard concentration result for Lipschitz function of Gaussian random variables
(
Borell, 1975
)
to bound
\|\psi(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{2}.

To accommodate the randomness in
\gamma_{t}\in\real^{t}
, we take a union bound over a covering set of
\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
of accuracy
\frac{1}{n}
.
Combining these ideas together, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, it satisfies

\displaystyle\Big\|\psi\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{2}-\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\psi(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]=O\Big(\|\varepsilon\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big).

(129)

It therefore leads to

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\psi(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]+O\Big(\|\varepsilon\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big).

(130)

Recall the definition of
\psi(z;\lambda)=\min\{\max\{z,-\lambda\},\lambda\}
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\psi(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\psi(g;\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}})\Big\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\sqrt{n\mathbb{E}\Big[\min\left\{\tilde{g}^{2},\lambda^{2}(1+b_{t})^{2}/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\Big]}.

(131)

Here, in the last equality, we denote
\tilde{g}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
.
Therefore in order to control
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}
, it suffices to bound the quantity
\mathbb{E}[\min\{\tilde{g}^{2},\lambda^{2}(1+b_{t})^{2}/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\}].

We claim that it satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\min\left\{\tilde{g}^{2},\lambda^{2}(1+b_{t})^{2}/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\Big]\leq(1-2c)^{2}\frac{p}{n^{2}}.

(132)

Putting everything together, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\leq(1-2c)\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}+O\Big(\|\varepsilon+\xi_{t}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big).

(133)

Combining the relation (
133
) with (
127
), we end up with

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\leq(1-c)\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}+o(1)<\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}

(134)

which is contradicted with
\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}\geq\overline{\gamma}>\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}
.
Hence, we conclude
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
for every
t\geq 1
,
which in turn leads to
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
by virtue of (
B.2.1
).

Proof of inequality (
132
).

First, we claim that the following relation holds true for
\tilde{g}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big)=\frac{p}{n}+o(1).

(135)

Let us take this relation as give for the moment and come back to its proof later.
Based on this, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{n\mathbb{E}\Big[\min\left\{\tilde{g}^{2},\lambda^{2}(1+b_{t})^{2}/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\Big]}{\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big)}\leq 1-5c,

for some constant
c
depending on
\frac{p}{n}
.
This can be seen from the numerical simulation in Figure
2
.
Here, we let
\tau:=\sqrt{n}\lambda(1+b_{t})/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}
and

\displaystyle H_{1}(\tau):=\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}\big(|G|>\tau\big)}\Big(1-\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[\min\left\{G^{2},\tau^{2}\right\}\big]}{\mathbb{P}\big(|G|<\tau\big)}\Big).

(136)

Putting these two things together finishes the proof of inequality (
132
).

Finally, we conclude by proving relation (
135
).
In view of the expression
r_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t}+\varepsilon
, first we make the observation that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\Big\|\ind\left(|r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\Big\|_{0}-\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{0}\Bigg|\leq\Bigg\|\ind\left(|r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)-\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Bigg\|_{1}.

Lemma
10
(
278b
) bounds the
\ell_{1}
discrepancy of
F_{t}^{\prime}
after pertrubing the input. By similar argument, we can derive perturbation results for
G_{t}^{\prime}
. In particular,
we bound

\displaystyle\Big\|\ind\left(|r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)-\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{1}\leq t\log n+n\Big(\frac{\|\xi_{t}+\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)^{2/3}.

(137)

Combining the above two inequalities yields

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\left(|r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\Big\|_{0}-\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{0}\Bigg|\leq\frac{t\log n}{n}+o(1),

(138)

where the last inequality uses the assumption
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}=o(1)
,
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}\asymp 1
, and the relation
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim\overline{\gamma}.

Now we move on to control the term
\frac{1}{n}\|\ind(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{0}.
Some direct algebra leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\|\ind\left(\frac{|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\right)\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}}\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}^{t-1})}\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\epsilon\Big)\Big\|_{0},

(139)

where
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}^{t-1})
forms an
\epsilon
-cover of
\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
.
Here in the last inequality, we make the observation that for every
\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
, there exists
\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}^{t-1})
such that
\|\zeta-\zeta^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
and hence

\displaystyle\Bigg||\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{\prime k}\phi_{kj}|-|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{kj}|\Bigg|\leq\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t}(\zeta^{k}-\zeta^{\prime k})\phi_{kj}\Big|\leq\|\zeta-\zeta^{\prime}\|_{2}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{t}\phi^{2}_{kj}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\epsilon,

(140)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.

Fix each
\zeta
independent of
\{\phi_{k}\}
,
\|\ind(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\epsilon)\|_{0}
is the summation of
n
independent Bernoulli distribution with parameter
\mathbb{P}(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\epsilon),
for
\tilde{g}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
.
According to standard concentration result for summation of independent Bernoulli’s, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\epsilon\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\epsilon\Big)+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\Big),

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
If we set
\epsilon=\frac{1}{n}
and take a union bound over elements in
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}^{t-1})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}^{t-1})}\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\epsilon\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}+\frac{1}{n}\Big)+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big),

(141)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.
Here the last inequality uses
P\Big(\lambda(1+b_{t})/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}<|\tilde{g}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}+1/n\Big)<\sqrt{2/(\pi n)}
since the density function of
|\tilde{g}|
is bounded by
\sqrt{2n/\pi}.

Combining with (
B.2.1
), the above relation leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{0}\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big).

(142)

Similarly, one can deduce

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\zeta\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}}\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}^{t-1})}\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\Big(|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}-\epsilon\Big)\Big\|_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big).

(143)

Putting these two parts with (
138
), we conclude

\displaystyle\frac{k_{t}}{n}=\frac{1}{n}\Big\|\ind\left(|r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\Big\|_{0}=\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)+o(1).

(144)

To establish (
135
), it is sufficient to notice that
k_{t}\asymp p
.
By definition of
k_{t}
(cf. (
124
)), it satisfies straightforwardly that
k_{t}>p.

According to (
144
), it then implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(|\tilde{g}|<\frac{\lambda(1+b_{t})}{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)\geq\frac{p}{n}+o(1).

(145)

Given
\tilde{g}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
, the above relation ensures

b_{t}\asymp\overline{\gamma}
for
\lambda\asymp 1/\sqrt{n}
and
\lambda(1+b_{t})/\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\asymp\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
, which in turns gives

\displaystyle k_{t}=p(1+1/b_{t})=p(1+o(1)).

We thus finish the proof of inequality (
135
).

Proof of
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2},\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim 1
.

We are only left to show
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim 1
.
Similar to (
B.2.1
), invoking the Lipschitz property of the function
\|\psi(\cdot\mid\lambda(1+b_{t}))\|_{2}
gives

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\left\|\psi\left(s_{t}+\varepsilon;\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{2}+O(\|\xi_{t}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Big\|_{2}+O(\|\xi_{t}\|_{2})

(146)

where we define
\mathcal{I}:=\{i:\varepsilon_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\}
.
Recall that
\varepsilon_{i}
is drawn from the mixture distribution of
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and some other distribution
H
as in (
39
).

In order to show
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim 1
, it suffices to lower bound
\|\psi(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t}))\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{2}
.
Before proceeding, we make two key observations.

•

We first make the remark that
\{\phi_{k}\}
are independent of
\varepsilon
.
In fact, when constructing
\{\phi_{k}\}
(cf. (
64
)), we have viewed
\varepsilon
as a deterministic vector and each
\phi_{k}
admits a fixed distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
no matter what value
\varepsilon
takes. In other words,
\{\phi_{k}\}
are independent of
\varepsilon
.

•

From our discussions around (
145
), it satisfies
\lambda(1+b_{t})\asymp\frac{\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Big\|_{2}\leq\|\lambda(1+b_{t})\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{2}\lesssim 1.

(147)

With these two facts in mind, similar to (
B.2.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\zeta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(S^{t-1})}\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\zeta^{k}\phi_{k}-\epsilon;\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\psi(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g-1/n;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\right),

(148)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
. Here we take
\epsilon=1/n.

Following the exact same argument for deriving (
88
), since
\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Big\|_{2}
concentrates tightly around its mean, and is bounded from above, one can deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g-1/n;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|^{2}_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\psi(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g-1/n;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]\Big)^{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\right).

(149)

When it comes to further bounding quantity
\mathbb{E}[\|\psi(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g-1/n;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\|^{2}_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}]
,
some direct algebra gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g-1/n;\lambda(1+b_{t}))\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|^{2}_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\min\big\{(\varepsilon_{k}+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g_{k}-1/n)^{2},~\lambda^{2}(1+b_{t})^{2}\big\}\Big]

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}(1+b_{t})^{2}\mathbb{P}\Big(\varepsilon_{k}>0,\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g_{k}-\frac{1}{n}>\frac{1}{2}\lambda(1+b_{t}),k\in\mathcal{I}\Big)

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1,

(150)

where in the last line, we recall the independence between
\varepsilon
and
\{\phi_{k}\}
and conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\varepsilon_{k}>0,\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g_{k}-\frac{1}{n}>\frac{1}{2}\lambda(1+b_{t}),k\in\mathcal{I}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\varepsilon_{k}>0\Big)\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g_{k}-\frac{1}{n}>\frac{1}{2}\lambda(1+b_{t}),k\in\mathcal{I}\Big)\gtrsim 1.

Putting together inequalities (
B.2.1
), (
B.2.1
) and (
B.2.1
)
concludes
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim 1
and thus
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\gtrsim 1.

Combining these two parts together, we have shown that
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2},\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1
, thus validating the Assumption
1
.

B.2.2
Validating Assumption
2

In view of the definition of (
45
), it is easily seen that
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{p}{n}
.
Therefore, in order to justify Assumption
2
, we are only left with computing

\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\|G_{t+1}^{\prime}(u_{t+1})\|_{2}^{2}]
.
Towards this, according to the choice of
b_{t}
(cf. (
124
)), we first make the observation that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|G_{t}^{\prime}(s_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\|g_{t}^{\prime}(r_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}=\Big(\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big)^{2}\cdot\frac{k_{t}}{n}=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}.

Next we shall compute the difference between
\|G_{t}^{\prime}(u_{t})\|_{2}^{2}
and
\|G_{t}^{\prime}(s_{t})\|_{2}^{2}
. Some direct algebra gives

\displaystyle\left|\left\|G_{t}^{\prime}(u_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|G_{t}^{\prime}(s_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big)^{2}\cdot\Big|\big\||\varepsilon+u_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\big\|_{0}-\big\||r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t})\big\|_{0}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big)^{2}\cdot\Big|\ind(\varepsilon+u_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t}))-\ind(|r_{t}|<\lambda(1+b_{t}))\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t\log n+n\Big(\frac{\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}}{\|\varepsilon+u_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{3}}\ll n.

Here the penultimate inequality results similarly from the relation (
137
) as a consequence of concentration lemma
4
; the last inequality invokes the assumption
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}=o(1)
and
inequality (
B.1.1
) that

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon+u_{t}\|_{2}=\Big\|\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}=\sqrt{\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\right)\left\|\gamma_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}\left\|\gamma_{t}\right\|_{2}\Big)}\gtrsim 1.

Combining the derivations above, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left\|G_{t}^{\prime}(u_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{p}\Big(1-\frac{1}{1+b_{t}}\Big)+o(1),

(151)

provided that
t\lesssim\frac{n}{\log^{4}n}
.
Putting everything together, we have verified that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\|G_{t+1}^{\prime}(u_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<1-\frac{1}{1+c^{\prime}\overline{\gamma}},

(152)

for some constant
c^{\prime}
. Here we recall
b_{t}\asymp\overline{\gamma}
as discussed around inequality (
145
).
We have thus validated Assumption
2
.

B.2.3
State evolution

Again, our final goal is to bound the difference between the non-asymptotic SE
(\alpha_{t},\gamma_{t+1})
to the deterministic SE defined in expression (
46
).
We proceed by using the induction method to achieve this goal.
Firstly, it is easily seen that the set of relation (
49a
) holds true for
t=1
. Assuming that for some
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\big|\|\alpha_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}-\alpha^{\star 2}_{t-1}\big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{1/3}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big|\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\gamma^{\star 2}_{t}\big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{1/3},

(153)

it is thus sufficient to verify them for
t+1
.

In the derivations above, we have shown that
\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2},\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1
. Based on these relations, we claim that

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{p}\Big(\frac{b_{t}}{1+b_{t}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi\big(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t})\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2},\varepsilon\Big]+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right).

(154)

Proof of relation (
154
).

First we recall inequality (
127
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{n}\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right).

(155)

The definition of
\alpha_{t}
directly yields

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}=\|G_{t}(s_{t})\|_{2}=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\left\|\psi\left(s_{t}+\varepsilon;\lambda(1+b_{t})\right)\right\|_{2}.

(156)

Next, in view of the Lipschitz property of the function
\psi
and the decomposition (
22a
) of
s_{t}
, we can further conclude

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\Big\|\psi\Big(\varepsilon+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\gamma_{t}^{k}\phi_{k};\lambda(1+b_{t})\Big)\Big\|_{2}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi\big(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t})\big)\big\|_{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2},\varepsilon\Big]+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right),

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10}).

Here we invoke the relation
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim O((\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n})^{\frac{1}{3}})
in the first line, and the concentration property of Gaussian vectors as in (
130
) in the second line.
In addition, since
\psi\big(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t})\big)
concentrates well around its expectation, with similar argument as in (
88
), one can derive

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{nb_{t}}{p(1+b_{t})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi\big(\varepsilon+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}g;\lambda(1+b_{t})\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\mid\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2},\varepsilon\Big]}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right),

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11}).
Putting the above together with (
155
), we establish the advertised relation (
154
).

Now based on the recursion (
154
), in order to study how
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}
evolves with
t
, it is sufficient to study the following function for any value
b>0
,

\displaystyle h_{b}(\mu):=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi\big(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}g;\lambda(1+b)\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\mid\varepsilon\Big].

(157)

With this definition, the limiting state-evolution (
46
) satisfies

\displaystyle\gamma_{t+1}^{\star 2}=\frac{n}{p}\Big(\frac{b_{t}^{\star}}{1+b_{t}^{\star}}\Big)^{2}h_{b_{t}^{\star}}(\gamma_{t}^{\star 2}),

(158)

while its non-asymptotic analogue satisfies

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{n}{p}\Big(\frac{b_{t}}{1+b_{t}}\Big)^{2}h_{b_{t}}(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2})+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right).

(159)

Now our goal is to control the difference between
\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\gamma_{t+1}^{\star 2}
. Towards this end, we first make note of the following two properties regarding the function
h_{b}
.

•

First, we claim that

\displaystyle\big|h_{b}^{\prime}(\mu)\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\psi\big(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}g;\lambda(1+b)\big),\ind(|\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}g|<\lambda(1+b))\circ g\big\rangle\mid\varepsilon\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-2c}{\mu}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\psi\big(\varepsilon+\sqrt{\mu}g;\lambda(1+b)\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\mid\varepsilon\Big]

(160)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-2c}{\mu}h_{b}(\mu).

Let us take inequality (
160
) as given for the moment, and come back to its proof at the end of this section.
Note that for
\mu\asymp 1
,
h_{b}(\mu)/{\mu}=O(1)
and the above property guarantees that
\frac{1}{\mu}h(\mu)
is
O(1)
-Lipschitz continuous function of
\mu.
As a result, one has

\displaystyle(1-2c)\frac{h_{b}(\mu)}{\mu}\leq(1-2c)\frac{h_{b}(\mu^{\prime})}{\mu^{\prime}}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\leq(1-c)\frac{h_{b}(\mu^{\prime})}{\mu^{\prime}},

for
|\mu-\mu^{\prime}|=O((\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n})^{\frac{1}{3}})
.
Putting the things above together yields

\displaystyle\big|h_{b}^{\prime}(\mu)\big|

\displaystyle\leq(1-c)\frac{h_{b}(\mu^{\prime})}{\mu^{\prime}},

(161)

for some constant
c\in(0,1)
,
b=b_{t},b_{t}^{\star}
and
|\mu-\mu^{\prime}|=O((\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n})^{\frac{1}{3}})
.

•

For any value of
\mu
, regarding
b^{2}h_{b}(\mu)/(1+b)^{2}
as a function of
b
, notice that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\big(b^{2}\psi(u/(1+b);\lambda)^{2}\big)}{\partial b}=\frac{2b\psi(u/(1+b);\lambda)^{2}}{1+b}\geq 0.

(162)

Therefore,
b^{2}h_{b}(\mu)/(1+b)^{2}
is a non-decreasing function of
b
.

With these properties in place, putting relations (
158
) and (
159
) together gives

\displaystyle\big|\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t+1}^{\star})^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{p}\max\bigg\{\Big(\frac{b_{t}^{\star}}{1+b_{t}^{\star}}\Big)^{2}\big|h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)\big|,\Big(\frac{b_{t}}{1+b_{t}}\Big)^{2}\big|h_{b_{t}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)\big|\bigg\}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big|\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big|+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)=o(1),

(163)

where the last relation invokes our inductive assumption. Furthermore, we can write

\displaystyle(\gamma_{t+1}^{\star})^{-2}\big|\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t+1}^{\star})^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\max\big\{\big(h_{b_{t}^{\star}}(\gamma_{t}^{\star 2})\big)^{-1}\big|h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)\big|,(1+o(1))\big(h_{b_{t}}(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2})\big)^{-1}\big|h_{b_{t}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)\big|\big\}+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-c)(\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{-2}\big|\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big|+O\left(\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right),

(164)

where the second line holds since for
b_{t}^{\star}\geq b_{t}
,

\displaystyle h_{b_{t}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)\leq h_{b_{t}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)\leq h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big(\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\big)-h_{b_{t}^{\star}}\big((\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big)

and the last line makes use of (
161
) with
\mu=c^{\prime}\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+(1-c^{\prime})(\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}
and
\mu^{\prime}=(\gamma_{t}^{\star})^{2}
for some
0\leq c^{\prime}\leq 1
.

In view of the initialization
\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{\star}_{1}=\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}
and
\|\gamma_{t}\|_{2}\asymp 1
, invoking the above relation (
B.2.3
) recursively leads to

\displaystyle\big|\|\gamma_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}-(\gamma_{t+1}^{\star})^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

which in turns gives that

\displaystyle\big|\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-(\alpha_{t}^{\star})^{2}\big|\lesssim\Big(\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Proof of Claim (
160
).

To establish the expression (
160
) for
b=b_{t}\text{ and }b^{\star}_{t}
,
consider a change of variable
\tau\coloneqq\frac{\sqrt{n}\lambda(1+b)}{\sqrt{\mu}}
.
It is then sufficient to prove that for any
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle H_{2}(\tau):=1-\sup_{\varepsilon}\frac{\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\varepsilon+G)\ind(|\varepsilon+G|<\tau)\Big]\Big|}{\mathbb{E}\big[(\varepsilon+G)^{2}\wedge\tau^{2}\big]}>c,

(165)

for some constant
c\in(0,1)
.

•

For
\tau\in(0,3)
, the required inequality (
165
) can be directly obtained from the numerical simulation in Figure
2
. Here the value
H_{2}(\tau)\geq 0.02
for
\tau\in(0,3)
.

•

For
\tau\geq 3
, due to symmetry, it is enough to consider the case
\varepsilon\geq 0
.
When
\varepsilon\geq\tau
, some direct calculations yield

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\varepsilon+G)\ind(|\varepsilon+G|<\tau)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\varepsilon+G)\ind(-\varepsilon-\tau<G<-\varepsilon)\Big],-\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\varepsilon+G)\ind(-\varepsilon<G<\tau-\varepsilon)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[G^{2}\ind(G<-\tau)\Big],-\tau\mathbb{E}\Big[G\ind(G<0)\Big]\Big\}\leq\tau,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\varepsilon+G)^{2}\wedge\tau^{2}\big]

\displaystyle>\tau^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\ind(G>0)\big]=\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}.

In this case,
H_{2}(\tau)\geq 1-\frac{2}{\tau}\geq 1/3.

In the other case, for
0\leq\varepsilon\leq\tau
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\varepsilon+G)\ind(|\varepsilon+G|<\tau)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\varepsilon+G)\ind(-\varepsilon-\tau<G<\varepsilon-\tau)\Big]\Big|+2\mathbb{E}\Big[G^{2}\ind(0<G<\tau-\varepsilon)\Big]

\displaystyle<\varepsilon\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[(\varepsilon+G)\ind(-\varepsilon-\tau<G<\varepsilon-\tau)\Big]\Big|+\mathbb{E}\Big[(\varepsilon+G)^{2}\ind(-\varepsilon-\tau<G<\varepsilon-\tau)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\Big[G^{2}\ind(0<G<\tau-\varepsilon)\Big]

\displaystyle<\tau^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\ind(G<\varepsilon-\tau)\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[(\varepsilon+G)^{2}\ind(-\varepsilon-\tau<G<\varepsilon-\tau)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\Big[G^{2}\ind(0<G<\tau-\varepsilon)\Big],

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\varepsilon+G)^{2}\wedge\tau^{2}\big]

\displaystyle>\tau^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\ind(G>\tau-\varepsilon)\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[(\varepsilon+G)^{2}\ind(-\varepsilon-\tau<G<\varepsilon-\tau)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\Big[(\varepsilon^{2}+G^{2})\ind(0<G<\tau-\varepsilon)\Big].

As a result,
H_{2}(\tau)>0.
Since we only need to consider those
\tau
such that
\tau\asymp 1
which forms a compact set, therefore it implies there exists some constant
c
such that

H_{2}(\tau)>c.

Figure 2:

Numerical calculations for
H_{1}(\tau)
of (
136
) with
\tau\in(0,5)

and
H_{2}(\tau)
of (
165
) with
\tau\in(0,3)
.

We have thus completed the proof of Claim (
160
).

Appendix C
Auxiliary concentration lemmas and their proofs

In this section, we collect a few concentration results for functions of random vectors that shall be used multiple times throughout this paper.

C.1
Lemma statements

The first result below considers the summation of independent sub-exponential random variables and develops a Bernstein-like concentration bound.

Lemma 3
.

Suppose that
Z_{i}
’s are independent random variables satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{P}\Big(|Z_{i}|\geq B\log\frac{1}{\delta}\Big)\leq\delta,\qquad\text{for every }0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)},

(166)

for some
B\geq 0.

Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, one has

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\mathsf{Var}(Z_{i})+\big(\frac{B\log n}{n}\big)^{2}\Big)\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+B\log n\log\frac{1}{\delta}.

(167)

The proof of this result can be found in Section
C.2
.

It is worth pointing out that a direct application of Bernstein’s inequality — in view of the boundedness condition (
166
) for each
Z_{i}
— adds an additional
\log\frac{1}{\delta}
to the second term of (
167
) (see e.g.
(
Wainwright, 2019
, Section 2.1.3)
).
In that case, this additional term when combined with a covering argument shall result in an inferior dependence on the parameter
t
.

Next, we derive a useful concentration bound associated with indicator functions.
In particular, we count the number of times that a Lipschitz function crosses a certain threshold over multiple independent realizations.
This concentration result turns out to be useful when dealing with discontinuous denoising functions with its proof postponed to Section
C.3
.

Lemma 4
.

Consider independent random vectors
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
Suppose for each
i\in[n]
,
h_{i}(x;\theta)
is a Lipschitz function w.r.t.
\theta\in\Theta
, with Lipschitz constant equals to
L
.
Additionally, assume that for any fixed
\theta
, there exists some
\sigma>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|h_{i}(X_{i};\theta)|<\frac{s\sigma}{n}\right)<\frac{s}{n},\qquad\forall s\in[n].

(168)

Then for every
\varepsilon\in\real^{n}
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, it obeys

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind(|h_{i}(X_{i};\theta)|<\varepsilon_{i})\lesssim\log N\Big(\frac{\sigma}{n^{2}},\Theta\Big)\log n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sigma}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}.

(169)

Finally, we conclude this section by summarizing some standard concentration results of independent Gaussian random vectors.

Again, denote a collection of independent Gaussian vectors
\{\phi_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq t}
and
\{\psi_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq t}
, with
\phi_{k}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
and
\psi_{k}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p})
.
If we concatenate
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t}
into a matrix
\Phi\in\real^{n\times t}
as

\displaystyle\Phi\coloneqq\big[\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{t}\big]\in\real^{n\times t},\qquad\text{where }\phi_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}),

(170)

its maximum singular value satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\Phi\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1+\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}+\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\leq e^{-\delta^{2}/2}.

(171)

In addition, for Wishart matrices, invoking the above result together with a union bound tells us that

\displaystyle\left\|(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{t-1})^{\top}(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{t-1})-I_{t-1}\right\|_{\textsf{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},\qquad\text{for every }1<t\leq n

(172a)

with probability at least
1-\delta
(see, also,
Wainwright, 2019
).
Similarly, for random vectors
\{\psi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t-1}
, independently drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p})
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{n}{p}(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{t-1})^{\top}(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{t-1})-I_{t-1}\right\|_{\textsf{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{p}{\delta}}{p}},\qquad\text{for every }1<t\leq n.

(172b)

For our convenience, we also recall the following lemma from
Li and Wei, 2022
. Here for every vector
x\in\real^{n}
, we follow the convention and write
|x|_{(i)}
as its
i
-th largest entry in magnitude.

Lemma 5
.

(
Li and Wei, 2022
, Lemma 8)

With probability at least
1-\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\max_{1\leq k\leq t-1}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},

(173a)

\displaystyle\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\bigg|\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},

(173b)

\displaystyle\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(t+s)\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n},\qquad\forall 1\leq s\leq n.

(173c)

For a set of random vectors
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t}
independently drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
,

C.2
Proof of Lemma
3

For every integer
k\geq 2
, let us consider the
k
-moment of random variable
Z_{i}
.
For notational convenience, define
Y_{i}\coloneqq\frac{Z_{i}}{B\log n}
and direct calculations yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{i}\right|^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{i}\right|^{k}\ind\left(\left|Z_{i}\right|\leq Bk\log n\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{i}\right|^{k}\ind\left(\left|Z_{i}\right|>Bk\log n\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{i})}}{{\lesssim}}(Bk\log n)^{k-2}\mathsf{Var}(Z_{i})+(Bk\log n)^{k}\exp(-k\log n)

\displaystyle\leq(Bk\log n)^{k}\left(\mathsf{Var}(Y_{i})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),

(174)

where the last step uses the definition of
Y_{i}
and
k\geq 2.

To verify the relation
(\mathrm{i})
, let us use
\mu_{\left|Z_{i}\right|}
to denote the density of
\left|Z_{i}\right|
.
By direct calculations, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{i}\right|^{k}\ind\left(\left|Z_{i}\right|>Bk\log n\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{Bk\log n}^{\infty}x^{k}\mu_{\left|Z_{i}\right|}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq(Bk\log n)^{k}\exp(-k\log n)+\int_{Bk\log n}^{\infty}\exp\Big(-\frac{x}{B}\Big)\mathrm{d}x^{k}

\displaystyle=(Bk\log n)^{k}\exp(-k\log n)+kB^{k}\int_{k\log n}^{\infty}x^{k-1}\exp(-x)\mathrm{d}x

(175)

where the first inequality invokes the condition (
166
).
To continue, invoking the rule of integration by part, the right hand side of (
175
) equals to

(
175
)

\displaystyle=(Bk\log n)^{k}\exp(-k\log n)+kB^{k}\exp(-k\log n)\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{(k-m)!}(k\log n)^{k-m}
