Combining the above two relations, we are guaranteed that

\displaystyle\left\|F_{t+1}\Big(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}\Big)\right\|_{2}\leq\|F_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}+\rho_{F}\|\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}\|_{2}\leq\rho_{F}\overline{\alpha}_{k}.

In addition, some direct algebra together with the Lipschitz property of function
F_{k}
leads to

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{t+1}-\widetilde{v}_{t+1}\|_{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(v_{k})-\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(\widetilde{v}_{k})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p}{n}\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{t\log p}{p}}\Big)\|\alpha_{t}-\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}-\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\|F_{k}(\widetilde{v}_{k})\|_{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\|F_{k}(v_{k})-F_{k}(\widetilde{v}_{k})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon+\epsilon\sum_{k=1}^{t}\rho_{F}\overline{\alpha}_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\rho_{F}\|v_{k}-\widetilde{v}_{k}\|_{2}.

(263)

Conditioning on event (
224
), for each
1\leq k\leq t
, one has

\displaystyle\|v_{k}-\widetilde{v}_{k}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{p}{n}\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{t\log p}{p}}\Big)\|\alpha_{k-1}-\widetilde{\alpha}_{k-1}\|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon,

(264)

which implies that (
263
) can be further bounded as

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\epsilon\sum_{k=1}^{t}\rho_{F}\overline{\alpha}_{k}+\epsilon\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\rho_{F}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{F}\overline{\alpha}_{t}.

(265)

Substituting the above relations into (
255
) establishes relation (
256
).

Appendix F
Proof of auxiliary lemmas

F.1
Proof of Lemma
1

To facilitate our analysis, let us first introduce some definitions and basic properties.
Recall that we define two sets of orthonormal basis
\{a_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq\min\{n,p\}}
and
\{b_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq\min\{n,p\}}
and for each
t
, concatenate them into orthonormal matrices

\displaystyle U_{t}=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}\in\real^{n\times t},\qquad V_{t}=[b_{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}\in\real^{p\times t}.

For every
1\leq k\leq\min\{n,p\}
, we write the orthogonal complement of
U_{k}
as
U_{k}^{\perp}\in\real^{n\times(n-k)}
which satisfies
U_{k}^{\top}U_{k}^{\perp}=0
and
U_{k}^{\perp\top}U_{k}^{\perp}=I_{n-k}.

Similarly, we write the orthogonal complement of
V_{k}
as
V_{k}^{\perp}\in\real^{p\times(p-k)}.

Additionally, we find it helpful to consider the projection where the rows of
X
are projected to the
p-k
-dimensional space
V_{k}^{\perp}
, and the columns to the
n-k
-dimensional space
U_{k}^{\perp}
, which is denoted by

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{k+1}\coloneqq U_{k}^{\perp\top}XV_{k}^{\perp}\in\real^{(n-k)\times(p-k)}.

(266)

With these notation in place,
X_{k+1}
(defined as in (
62
)) obeys

\displaystyle X_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\left(I_{n}-a_{k}a_{k}^{\top}\right)X_{k}\left(I_{p}-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top}\right)=\cdots=\left(I_{n}-U_{k}U_{k}^{\top}\right)X\left(I_{p}-V_{k}V_{k}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=U_{k}^{\perp}U_{k}^{\perp\top}XV_{k}^{\perp}V_{k}^{\perp\top}=U_{k}^{\perp}\widetilde{X}_{k+1}V_{k}^{\perp\top}.

Claim 3
.

For every
1\leq k\leq\min\{n,p\}
, conditional on
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
,
the following properties hold true:

•

\widetilde{X}_{k+1}
is a rescaled Wigner matrix in
(n-k)×(p-k)
, with
(\widetilde{X}_{k+1})_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
;

•

X_{k+1}
is conditional independent of
\{X_{i}b_{i},X_{i}^{\top}a_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k};

•

the randomness of
(s_{k},\beta_{k+1})
and
\{a_{k+1},b_{k+1}\}
comes purely from
\{X_{i}b_{i},X_{i}^{\top}a_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
,
and hence
(s_{k},\beta_{k+1})
and
\{a_{k+1},b_{k+1}\}
are conditionally independent of
W_{k+1}
.

In view of relations (
A.1
) and (
69
), the proof of Claim
3
proceeds by the same induction method as in the proof of
(
Li and Wei, 2022
, Claim 1)
. We thus omit its details here.

Equipped with the results above, let us characterize the distribution of
W_{k}b_{k}
for
2\leq k\leq\min\{n,p\}.
To begin with, conditional on
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle a_{i}^{\top}X_{k}b_{k}

\displaystyle=a_{i}^{\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp}\widetilde{X}_{k}V_{k-1}^{\perp\top}b_{k}=0\qquad\text{for }i\leq k-1;

\displaystyle U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}X_{k}b_{k}

\displaystyle=(U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp})\widetilde{X}_{k}(V_{k-1}^{\perp\top}b_{k})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n-k+1}\right),

where we make use of the fact that
\widetilde{X}_{k}
is a rescaled Wigner matrix in
(n-k+1)×(p-k+1)
conditionally independent of
b_{k}
.
As a consequence, if one generates i.i.d. Gaussian random variables
g_{k}^{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
for all
1\leq i<k
, then conditional on
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
,
it follows that

\displaystyle\phi_{k}

\displaystyle=X_{k}b_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g^{i}_{k}a_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\right).

(267)

Similarly, one can characterize the distribution of
a_{k}^{\top}X_{k}(I_{p}-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top})
by noticing

\displaystyle a_{k}^{\top}X_{k}(I_{n}-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top})b_{k}

\displaystyle=0;

\displaystyle a_{k}^{\top}X_{k}(I_{p}-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top})b_{i}

\displaystyle=a_{k}^{\top}X_{k}b_{i}=a_{k}^{\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp}\widetilde{X}_{k}V_{k-1}^{\perp\top}b_{i}=0\qquad\text{for }i\leq k-1;

\displaystyle a_{k}^{\top}X_{k}(I_{p}-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top})V^{\perp}_{k}

\displaystyle=a_{k}^{\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp}\widetilde{X}_{k}V_{k-1}^{\perp\top}(I_{p}-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top})V^{\perp}_{k}=(a_{k}^{\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp})\widetilde{X}_{k}(V_{k-1}^{\perp\top}V^{\perp}_{k})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n-k}\right).

Here the last relation follows since conditioning on
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
,
\widetilde{X}_{k}
is a rescaled Wigner matrix in
(n-k+1)×(p-k+1)
independent of
(a_{k},b_{k})
.
It thus obeys that

\displaystyle\psi_{k}=\left(I-b_{k}b_{k}^{\top}\right)X_{k}^{\top}a_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}q^{i}_{k}b_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p}\right).

Finally, we make the observation that
\{\phi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
are independent, so as
\{\psi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
. In order to see this, first note that each
\phi_{k}
is independent of
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
which follows immediately from the conditional distributional guarantee established in (
267
). Next, putting together Claim
3
with the definition of
\phi_{k}
implies that conditional on
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
,
\phi_{k}
— whose randomness comes purely from
X_{k}b_{k}
and
g^{i}_{k}
— is statistically independent of
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1}
. Again, as the distribution of
\phi_{k}
does not relies on
\{a_{i},b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
and
(s_{0},\beta_{1})
, therefore, we conclude
\{\phi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
are statistically independent.
Similarly, one can also validate
\{\psi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
are statistically independent.

F.2
Proof of Lemma
8

To control quantity
\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\|_{2}^{2}
with
\omega\perp\alpha_{t}
(see (
205
)), the idea is to invoke Lemma
3
for any fixed
\omega\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
— independent of
v_{t+1}

and apply a standard covering argument.
Given any fixed
\alpha_{t}
and
\omega\perp\alpha_{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
,
\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}
follows
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
, which is independent with
v_{t+1}\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big].

Recognizing that
|F_{t+1}^{\prime}|\leq\rho_{F}
, it can be easily verified via properties for Gaussian distribution that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Big)_{i}^{4}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\rho_{F}^{4},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in[n]}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}\left(v_{t+1}\right)\Big)_{i}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{F}^{2}}{n}\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

In view of Lemma
3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\rho_{F}^{2}+\frac{\rho_{F}^{2}}{n}\log^{2}n\log\frac{1}{\delta},

(268)

which holds with probability at least
1-\delta.

To take care of the statistical dependence between
\omega,\alpha_{t}
and
\psi_{k}
, let us consider an
\epsilon
-cover of
\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
in terms of the
\ell_{2}
-norm, denoted by
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
. With this definition, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\perp\alpha\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}}~\Bigg\{\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\omega\perp\alpha\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}~\Bigg\{\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\Bigg\}+\mathsf{poly}(n)\cdot\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\omega\perp\alpha\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}~\Bigg\{\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\Bigg\}+\mathsf{poly}(n)\cdot\epsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\Big(\frac{N(\epsilon,\mathcal{S}^{t-1})}{\delta}\Big)}{n}}\rho_{F}^{2}+\frac{\rho_{F}^{2}}{n}\log^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{N(\epsilon,\mathcal{S}^{t-1})}{\delta}\Big)+\mathsf{poly}(n)\cdot\epsilon,

where the last inequality holds with probability
1-\delta
and the second inequality follows from that conditioning on the event in (
172b
),
\|v_{t+1}-\widetilde{v}_{t+1}\|_{2}\leq\mathsf{poly}(n)\cdot\epsilon
and
\big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}(w_{k}-\widetilde{w}_{k})\psi_{k}\big\|_{2}\leq\mathsf{poly}(n)\cdot\epsilon
.
Selecting parameters

\displaystyle\delta=\frac{1}{n^{10}}\qquad\text{and }~\epsilon=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)},

gives

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}}~\Bigg\{\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\Bigg\}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}}\rho_{F}^{2},

which completes the proof of the targeted bound (
213a
).
Following similar argument, one can also derive inequality (
213b
).

F.3
Proof of Lemma
9

Recalling the definition in expression (
227
), we begin by directly decomposing the quantity of interest as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{Var}(X_{i}^{0}+X_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\circ F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\circ F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log n}{n}\mathbb{E}\left\|F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\circ F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(269)

Next, we control these two parts above separately.

•

Regarding the first part, following by the Lipschitz property of
F_{t+1}
and relation (
E.1
), it satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|F_{t+1}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}+\rho_{F}^{2}\Big\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}^{2}+\rho_{F}^{2}\Big\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big\|_{2}^{2}.

(270)

Here, recall
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}\coloneqq v_{t+1}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(v_{k})
and
v_{k+1}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}
to obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\Big\|v_{t+1}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(v_{k})-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|v_{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\rho_{F}^{2}\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}v_{k}|\Big\|_{2}^{2},

(271)

where the last inequality again invokes the Lipschitz property of
F_{k}
.
Taking the expectation on both sides, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\rho_{F}^{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{i}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{j}|\cdot\mathbb{E}[\left\|v_{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|v_{j}\right\|_{2}].

(272)

Here, again, we remind the readers that
\widehat{\gamma}_{t}
is regarded as a fixed parameter.
Now in order to bound the right hand side of (
272
), since
v_{i+1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\|\alpha_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}I_{p})
for every fixed
\alpha_{i}
, it obeys that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{\|v_{i+1}\|_{2}}{\|\alpha_{i}\|_{2}}\frac{\|v_{j+1}\|_{2}}{\|\alpha_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\max\{\|X\|_{2}^{2},\|Y\|_{2}^{2}\}\Big]\qquad\text{where }X,Y\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|Y\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{p}{n}.

Therefore, the right hand side of (
272
) further satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(0)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{p\rho_{F}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i,j=0}^{t-1}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{i}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{j}|\|\alpha_{i}\|_{2}\|\alpha_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\rho_{F}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}\|\alpha_{k-1}\|_{2}\right)^{2}\lesssim\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}.

Combining with (
•
‣
F.3
) ensures

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}^{2}+\rho_{F}^{2}\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}.

(273)

Thus, we complete the control of the first term in (
269
).

•

It then suffices to control the second term, which shall again be done by means of concentration of measure.
We claim that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\circ F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}(\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}^{2}+\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}).

(274)

In order to see this, first, by putting together inequalities (
•
‣
F.3
), (
271
) and (
E.1
), we have

\displaystyle\Big\|F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}+\|v_{t+1}\|_{2}+\rho_{F}\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}v_{k}|\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}+\|v_{t+1}\|_{2}+\rho_{F}\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\|v_{k}\|_{2}\leq\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}+2\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}\overline{\alpha}_{t},

where the last inequality uses the fact that for each
k
,
v_{k+1}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}
and
\rho_{F}\sum_{k=1}^{t}|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\ll 1.

In view of this relation, we can decompose the quantity of interest as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\circ F_{t+1}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{t+1}^{2}}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}+\overline{\alpha}_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\right].

(275)

Here, the last inequality uses the property that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[X^{2}_{i}\ind\Big(X_{i}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\Bigg]\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)},\qquad\text{for }X_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}\Big),

(276)

and given a fixed vector
\omega\in\mathcal{S}^{t}
,
\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{p})
.
We now turn to the upper bound of the second term on the right of expression (
275
).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\ind(\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}-1\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\ind\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\ind(\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}-1\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{i})}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\ind(\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}-1\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{ii})}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)},

where (i) results from relation (
276
) and (ii) follows from the concentration result for
\|\Psi\|_{\mathrm{op}}
(cf. (
171
)).

Finally, combining relations (
273
) and (
274
) leads to our target bound.

F.4
Covering lemmas

As defined around display (
244
), for every
\widehat{\theta}=\Big(\omega,\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k\leq t},\widehat{\gamma}_{t},\{\tau_{k}\}_{k\leq t+1}\Big)\in\Theta
,
\widetilde{\theta}\coloneqq\Big(\widetilde{\omega},\{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}\}_{k\leq t},\widetilde{\gamma}_{t},\{\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\}_{k\leq t+1}\Big)
is a point that lies in the
\epsilon
-cover
\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon}
of
\Theta_{0}
which satisfies

\displaystyle\|\omega-\widetilde{\omega}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon,\quad\|\alpha_{k}-\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon,\quad\|\tau_{k}-\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon,\quad|\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}-\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}^{k}|\leq\epsilon,

for every
k
and
\epsilon=1/\mathsf{poly}(n).
We also record that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}^{k}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{for }k\leq t-O(\log n),\\
\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}&\text{o.w.}\end{cases}

Lemma 10
.

Under the assumptions (
191b
) – (
188
), the following set of relations holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})

•

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(\widetilde{v}_{j}\right)\right\rangle\right|\,\lesssim\,\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}\rho_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{F}\rho_{1,F}\overline{\alpha}_{t},

(277a)

\displaystyle\left|\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle-\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1})\rangle\right|\,\lesssim\,\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}\rho_{F}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{1,F}\overline{\alpha}_{t};

(277b)

•

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\left[F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1}+\varepsilon)-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right]\right\|_{2}\,\lesssim\,\rho_{1,F}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\varepsilon\|_{2}+\rho_{F}\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}}+\sqrt{\log n}\Big(\frac{\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Bigg),

(278a)

\displaystyle\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{1}\,\lesssim\,\sqrt{n}\rho_{1,F}\left\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-v_{t+1}\right\|_{2}+\rho_{F}\Bigg(t\log n+n\Big(\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-v_{t+1}\|_{2}}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{3}}\Bigg),

(278b)

\displaystyle\left|\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\beta_{t+1})\rangle\right|\,\lesssim\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\rho_{1,F}\left\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\beta_{t+1}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n}\rho_{F}\Bigg(t\log^{2}n+n\Big(\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\beta_{t+1}\|_{2}}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{3}}\Bigg);

(278c)

•

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\,\lesssim\,\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}\rho_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{1,F}\rho_{F},

(279a)

\displaystyle\left|\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\,\lesssim\,\rho_{F}^{2}t\log^{2}n+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{1,F}\rho_{F}.

(279b)

The proof of this lemma is provided in Section
F.5
.

F.5
Proof of Lemma
10

Before diving into details, let us first describe a general framework for bounding the fluctuation of a function when its input is perturbed slightly.
Validating each inequality of Lemma
10
then boils down to computing specific parameters in the general framework.
Throughout this proof, we condition on the event where both (
172a
) and (
172b
) satisfy with
\delta
selected as
\max(n,p)^{-11}
.

F.5.1
A general framework

Let us first set up the stage.
The multivariate mapping and its perturbation that we are interested in are of the form

\displaystyle H(x,\theta)=\Big[c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)h_{i}(u_{i}(x_{i},\theta))\Big]_{i=1}^{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_{\varepsilon}(x,\theta)=\Big[c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)h_{i}(u_{i}(x_{i},\theta)+\varepsilon_{i})\Big]_{i=1}^{n},

for perturbation vector
\varepsilon\in\real^{n}
and parameter
\theta\in\real^{d}.

Here
c_{i}
and
u_{i}
denote
\mathsf{poly}(n)
-Lipschitz continuous functions of
\theta
and
h_{i}
stands for functions with finite jump points.
Specifically, consider functions
h_{i}
that can be decomposed into a continuous component and a discontinuous component

\displaystyle h_{i}(u)=h_{i}^{\mathsf{cont}}(u)+h_{i}^{\mathsf{dis}}(u).

(280)

We assume the continuous part of
h_{i}
is
L
-Lipschitz and the discontinuous component takes the form

\displaystyle h_{i}^{\mathsf{dis}}(u):=\sum_{k=1}^{M_{i}}s_{i}^{k}\ind(u>\tau_{i}^{k}).

Here for each
i\in[n]
, we denote the discontinuous points of
h_{i}
as
\{\tau_{i}^{k}\}_{k=1}^{M_{i}}
, and the size of their jumps as
\{s_{i}^{k}\}_{k=1}^{M_{i}}
.

Given every
x
and
\varepsilon
, in order to compute the difference between
H(x,\theta)
and
H_{\varepsilon}(x,\theta)
, it is critical to track where
h_{i}^{\mathsf{dis}}(u)
and
h_{i}^{\mathsf{dis}}(u+\varepsilon)
differ.
For this purpose, let us define the index set

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}:=\Big\{i:\ind(u_{i}(x_{i},\theta)>\tau_{i}^{k})\neq\ind(u_{i}(x_{i},\theta)+\varepsilon_{i}>\tau_{i}^{k})\text{ for some }k\Big\}.

In words,
h_{i}^{\mathsf{dis}}(u_{i})=h_{i}^{\mathsf{dis}}(u_{i}+\varepsilon_{i})
for all
i\in[n]
, on set
\mathcal{I}.

In terms of this notation, the Lipschitz property of
h^{\mathsf{cont}}_{i}
ensures that

\displaystyle\|H(x,\theta)-H_{\varepsilon}(X,\theta)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}B|c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)|+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}L|c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)\varepsilon_{i}|,

(281a)

\displaystyle\|H(x,\theta)-H_{\varepsilon}(X,\theta)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}B^{2}|c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)|^{2}+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}L^{2}|c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)\varepsilon_{i}|^{2},

(281b)

provided that
|h_{i}(x_{i},\theta)|\lesssim B
for every
i
.

Additionally, consider mappings

\displaystyle H^{j}(x,\theta)=\Big[h_{i}^{j}(u_{i}^{j}(x_{i},\theta))\Big]_{i=1}^{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_{\varepsilon}^{j}(x,\theta)=\Big[h_{i}^{j}(u_{i}^{j}(x_{i},\theta)+\varepsilon_{i}^{j})\Big]_{i=1}^{n},

for
j=1
or
2.

Under the assumption
|h_{i}^{j}|\lesssim B
, in view of the Lipschitz property for the continuous part of
h_{i}^{j}
, we can conclude similarly that

\displaystyle\|H^{1}(x,\theta)\circ H^{2}(x,\theta)-H_{\varepsilon^{1}}^{1}(x,\theta)\circ H_{\varepsilon^{2}}^{2}(x,\theta)\|_{1}\lesssim\sum_{i\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}}B^{2}+\sum_{i\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}}LB(|\varepsilon_{i}^{1}|+|\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|),

(281c)

for the index set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}:=\Big\{i:\ind(u_{i}^{j}(x_{i},\theta)>\tau_{i}^{j,k})\neq\ind(u_{i}^{j}(x_{i},\theta)+\varepsilon_{i}^{j}>\tau_{i}^{j,k})\text{ for some }j,k\Big\}.

We shall employ these three relations above to establish Lemma
10
, which boils down to compute the right hand side of each inequality in (
281
).
Towards this goal, the idea is to apply the concentration results developed in Section
C
.
Below, we state two key observations and then turn to the calculations of each inequality individually.

Consider a random vector
X\in\real^{n}
. For any fixed
\theta\in\Theta
, suppose there exists some
\sigma>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|u^{j}_{i}(X_{i};\theta)|<\frac{s\sigma}{n}\right)<\frac{s}{n},

(282)

for every
s\in[n]
and
j=1,2
if there are two sets of
u_{i}^{j}
concerned.
In view of Lemma
4
and (
282
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}|\lesssim\log N\Big(\frac{\sigma}{100n^{2}},\Theta\Big)\log n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sigma}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},

(283)

where
N(\frac{\sigma}{100n^{2}},\Theta)
denotes the covering number of
\Theta
.

In addition, suppose that for every fixed
\theta
and
i\in[n]
,
c_{i}(x_{i};\theta)
is sub-exponential with

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(|c_{i}(x_{i};\theta)|\leq M\log\frac{1}{\delta}\Big)\geq 1-\delta,

(284)

for some
M>0
. It is easily seen that
\mathbb{E}\big[|c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)|\big]\lesssim M
and
\mathsf{Var}\big(|c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)|\big)\lesssim M^{2}.

Conditioning on the cardinality of
\mathcal{I}
, let us consider the quantity
\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)
where
w\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
and
\|w\|_{1}=|\mathcal{I}|
.
For every fixed
w
and
\theta\in\Theta
, by virtue of Lemma
3
, it holds true that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)\leq M|\mathcal{I}|+\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\big(c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)-\mathbb{E}\big[w_{i}(x_{i},\theta)\big]\big)\lesssim M|\mathcal{I}|+M\sqrt{|\mathcal{I}|\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+M\log n\log\frac{1}{\delta}

(285)

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
Here we make use of the following relations

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[w_{i}(x_{i},\theta)\big]\lesssim M\qquad\text{and }~\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{Var}\big(s_{i}c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)\big)\lesssim M^{2}|\mathcal{I}|.

Note that (
285
) holds true for every fixed
w\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
and
\theta\in\Theta
. In order to accommodate the possible statistical dependences, we consider an
\epsilon
-cover of
\Theta
.
Selecting parameters

\displaystyle\delta=\frac{1}{n^{11}N(\epsilon,\Theta)2^{|\mathcal{I}|}{n\choose|\mathcal{I}|}},\quad\epsilon=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}

and taking union bound of (
285
) over possible choices of
w
and
\theta\in\Theta
give

\displaystyle\sup_{\widehat{\theta}\in\Theta}~\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|c_{i}(x_{i},\widehat{\theta})|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\theta}\in\Theta,w\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{m}\\
\|w\|_{1}=|\mathcal{I}|\end{subarray}}~\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{i})}}{{\leq}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon},w\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{m}\\
\|w\|_{1}=|\mathcal{I}|\end{subarray}}~\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}

\displaystyle\lesssim M|\mathcal{I}|\log n+M\log N\Big(\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)},\Theta\Big)\log^{2}n,

(286)

where (i) follows from the choice of
\epsilon
and the Lipschitz property of each
c_{i}
.

F.5.2
Validating inequalities of Lemma
10

To validate Lemma
10
, we follow the general recipe provided above for specific choices of functions
h_{i}
,
u_{i}
and
c_{i}
.
In particular, we shall select
h_{i}
as either
F_{t+1,i}^{\prime}
or
(F_{t+1,i}^{\prime})^{2}
,
u_{i}
as either
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1,i}
or
v_{t+1,i}
, and
c_{i}(x_{i},\theta)
as
1
,
\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i}
, or
(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i})^{2}
.

As discussed above, we make note of the following observations:

•

Inequality set (
281
) requires a uniform bound
B
for function
h_{i}
, in which case, we can take
B=\rho_{F}
when

h_{i}=F_{t+1,i}^{\prime}
and
B=\rho^{2}_{F}
when
(F_{t+1,i}^{\prime})^{2}
.

•

For assumption (
284
),
M
can be set as
1
,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
, and
\frac{1}{n}
, respectively;

•

Regarding assumption (
282
), it suffices to select
\sigma
parameter as
\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}
for both

\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
and
v_{t+1}
. We leave the proof of this fact to the end of this section.

Proof of inequality (
277a
).

The idea is to apply inequality (
281c
) for proper choices of
H^{1}
and
H^{2}
.
Specifically, set

\displaystyle H^{1}(\Psi,\theta)\coloneqq F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1})\quad\text{and }H^{2}(\Psi,\theta)\coloneqq F_{j}^{\prime}\left(\widetilde{v}_{j}\right),

and

\displaystyle H_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\Psi,\theta)\coloneqq F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\quad\text{and }H_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\Psi,\theta)\coloneqq F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right).

With these choices in mind,
\varepsilon^{1}=\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}
,
\varepsilon^{2}=v_{j}-\widetilde{v}_{j}
, and they satisfy

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{F}\overline{\alpha}_{t},\qquad\|\varepsilon^{2}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)},

(287)

in view of relations (
264
) and (
265
).
Then according to (
281c
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(\widetilde{v}_{j}\right)\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}\right)-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1})\circ F_{j}^{\prime}\left(\widetilde{v}_{j}\right)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\rho_{F}^{2}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}|+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{F}\rho_{1,F}\overline{\alpha}_{t}.

To establish inequality (
277a
), it suffices to bound the cardinality of
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}
which shall be done via inequality (
283
).
Specifically, inequality (
283
) requires bounding the covering number of the space
\{(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1},\widetilde{v}_{j})\}
.

Recall the definitions

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\widetilde{a}_{j-1}^{i}\psi_{k}\quad\text{and }~\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(\widetilde{v}_{k}).

For every
\widetilde{\theta}=(\widetilde{\omega},\{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t},\widetilde{\gamma}_{t},\{\widetilde{\tau}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{t+1})
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}^{\prime}=(\widetilde{\omega}^{\prime},\{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\prime}\}_{k=1}^{t},\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}^{\prime},\{\widetilde{\tau}_{j}^{\prime}\}_{j=1}^{t+1}),

where
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}
for
k>t-O(\log n)
,
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\prime}=0
for
k\leq t-O(\log n)
.
It is proved in (
248
) that
\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}=\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1}^{\prime}
. Therefore to construct a
\epsilon
-cover for space
\{(\widetilde{\beta}_{t+1},\widetilde{v}_{j})\}
, it is sufficient to consider a
\epsilon
cover for
\widetilde{a}_{j-1}
together with
\widetilde{\theta}^{\prime}
. The total dimension is of order
t\log n
.
As a result, inequality (
283
) gives

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}|\lesssim t\log^{3}n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sigma}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\lesssim t\log^{3}n.

(288)

Here
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})
satisfies inequality (
287
), and
\sigma=\sqrt{\frac{2}{n\pi}}\overline{\alpha}_{t}.

The last relation follows from assumption (
191b
) that

\overline{\alpha}_{t}\leq\mathsf{poly}(n)
.

Putting things together completes the proof of inequality (
277a
).
Inequality (
277b
) is a direct consequence of inequality (
277a
) by directly setting
H^{2}(\Psi,\theta)=H_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\Psi,\theta)=1.

Proof of inequality (
278a
).

In order to prove inequality (
278a
), let us take
c_{i}=(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k})_{i}
,

\displaystyle H(\Psi,\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})

\displaystyle\text{ and }H_{\varepsilon}(\Psi,\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1}+\varepsilon).

Then according to (
281b
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\left[F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1}+\varepsilon)-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right]\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\rho_{F}^{2}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i}\Big|^{2}+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}\rho_{1,F}^{2}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i}\varepsilon_{i}\Big|^{2}.

(289)

We control each term of the right hand side of (
289
) respectively.
To begin with, the parameter that we shall build a
\epsilon
-cover with is
v_{t+1}
which is determined by
\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\in\real^{t}
.
In view of inequality (
283
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}|\lesssim\log N\Big(\frac{\sigma}{100n^{2}},\Theta\Big)\log n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sigma}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\lesssim t\log^{2}n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|/\sqrt{n}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},

(290)

where we recall the
\sigma
parameter for
v_{t+1}
equals to
\sqrt{\frac{2}{n\pi}}\overline{\alpha}_{t}.

In view of the relation (
173c
) in Lemma
5
, one has

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{t\log n}{n},~\text{ and }~\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i}\Big|^{2}\lesssim\frac{(t+|\mathcal{I}|)\log n}{n},

(291)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10}).

Taking everything collectively, we arrive at

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ\left[F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1}+\varepsilon)-F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right]\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{F}^{2}\left(\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}+\log n\left(\frac{\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}}{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)+\rho_{1,F}^{2}\frac{t\log n}{n}\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2},

from which the advertised claim in (
278a
) follows.
The proofs of (
278b
) and (
278c
) can be established in the same manner, by invoking relation (
281a
) with
c_{i}=1
and
h_{i}=F^{\prime}_{t+1,i}
. Here
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-v_{t+1}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}-\beta_{t+1}
play the role of
\varepsilon
in these cases.

Proof of inequality (
279a
).

To establish inequality (
279a
), consider
c_{i}=(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k})^{2}_{i}
and

\displaystyle H(\Psi,\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right)^{2}\in\real^{p},

\displaystyle H_{\varepsilon}(\Psi,\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right)^{2}\in\real^{p}.

By virtue of (
264
),
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}=\|\widetilde{v}_{t+1}-v_{t+1}\|_{2}\lesssim\epsilon=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}
.
Some basic algebra leads to

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right)^{2}-\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right)^{2}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\rho_{F}^{2}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i}\Big|^{2}+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}\rho_{1,F}\rho_{F}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k,i}\Big|^{2}|\varepsilon_{i}|

where the last line follows from relation (
281a
).
Similar to the discussions around display (
290
), inequality (
283
) gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}|\lesssim\log N\Big(\frac{\sigma}{100n^{2}},\Theta\Big)\log n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sigma}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\lesssim t\log^{2}n+\left(\frac{n\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|/\sqrt{n}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\lesssim t\log^{2}n,

where the last inequality invokes
\|\varepsilon\|\lesssim\epsilon=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}
.
Taking this together with concentration bounds in (
291
) further leads to

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}\rho_{F}^{2}+\frac{t^{2}\log^{2}n}{n^{2}}\rho_{1,F}\rho_{F}\|\varepsilon\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n}\rho_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{1,F}\rho_{F}.

(292)

Contrasting the above to our target bound (
279a
), we are only left to consider replacing
\omega_{k}
to
\widetilde{\omega}_{k}
in the second term of the left hand side.
Specifically, note that

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\left|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\Big)^{\top}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p}\rho_{F}\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{\infty}\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}(\omega_{k}-\widetilde{\omega}_{k})\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p}\rho_{F}\frac{t\log n}{n}\cdot\rho_{F}\frac{p}{n}\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{p}}\Big)\|\omega-\widetilde{\omega}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{F}^{2},

(293)

where for the penultimate line, recall that we condition on the event (
172b
) with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
; the last line invokes the assumption that
\|\omega-\widetilde{\omega}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon.

Combined with inequality (
F.5.2
), the above relation implies that

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(v_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\omega_{k}\psi_{k}\circ F_{t+1}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{t+1})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(n)}\rho_{1,F}\rho_{F}+\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\rho_{F}^{2}.

We thus complete the proof of inequality (
279a
).
Similarly, by taking
c_{i}=1
for every
i\in[n]
, inequality (
279b
) follows by the same argument above immediately.

The remaining terms can be proved in a similar way, which is omitted here for simplicity.

F.5.3
Other auxiliary details

\sigma
-parameter for
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
and
v_{j+1}
.

Recall that
{v}_{j+1}=\sum_{k=1}^{j}\widehat{\alpha}_{j}^{i}\psi_{k}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}({v}_{k}).

Given every fixed
\theta\in\Theta
and
i\in[n]
, by definition, each
v_{j+1,i}
follows
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}}{n})
, and hence, the density function of
|v_{j+1,i}|
is uniformly bounded by
\sqrt{\frac{2}{n\pi}}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{j}\|_{2}.

Therefore, it is sufficient to set
\sigma=\sqrt{\frac{2}{n\pi}}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{j}\|_{2}
for assumption (
282
).

Additionally, the quantity of interest
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
yields the following decomposition

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=v_{t+1}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(v_{k})=v_{t+1}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}F_{k}(v_{k}^{\parallel}+v_{k}^{\perp})

(294)

\displaystyle\text{where }\qquad v_{k}^{\parallel}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{k}^{\top}v_{t+1}}{\|v_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}}v_{t+1},\quad v_{k}^{\perp}=v_{k}-v_{k}^{\parallel},

where
v_{k}^{\parallel}
denotes the component that aligns with
v_{t+1}
while

v_{k}^{\perp}
denotes the component that is orthogonal to
v_{t+1}.

As discussed previously, given every fixed
\theta
, each
{v}_{k}
follows a Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}I_{p})
, therefore
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
is a function of Gaussian vectors.
In addition, we make the following observation that

\displaystyle\frac{|v_{k}^{\top}v_{t+1}|}{\|v_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\widehat{\alpha}_{k-1}^{j}\psi_{j}\Big)^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{j}\psi_{j}\Bigg|\cdot\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{j}\psi_{j}\Big\|_{2}^{-2}

\displaystyle=\Big|(\widehat{\alpha}_{k-1}^{1},\ldots,\widehat{\alpha}_{k-1}^{k-1},0,\ldots,0)\Psi^{\top}\Psi\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\Big|\cdot(\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{\top}\Psi^{\top}\Psi\widehat{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p}{n}\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{p}{\delta}}{p}}\Big)\|\widehat{\alpha}_{k-1}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{n}{p}\Big(1-\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{p}{\delta}}{p}}\Big)^{-1}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}^{-2}\lesssim\frac{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{k-1}\|_{2}}{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}}\leq\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{k-1}}{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}}.

Recalling the assumption (
191c
) that
\rho_{F}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\widehat{\gamma}_{t}^{k}\overline{\alpha}_{t}\ll\|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}\|_{2}
,
it therefore implies that conditioning on any value of
v_{t+1}^{\perp}
,
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1}
is a Lipschitz function of
v_{t+1}
with Lipschitz constant of order
1
.
As a result, for every
i\in[n]
and interval
\mathcal{I}_{i}
of length
\varepsilon
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{v_{t+1}}\Big(\frac{1}{\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}}\widehat{\beta}_{t+1,i}\in\mathcal{I}_{i}~\big|~v_{t+1}^{\perp}\Big)\lesssim\varepsilon,

by noticing that
\widehat{\beta}_{t+1,i}
is a
\Theta(1)
-Lipschitz function of
v_{t+1,i}
.
This implies that assumption (
282
) holds with
\sigma=\|\alpha_{t}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}.
